} }\\n@import \\\"tailwindcss\\\";\\n@import \\\"../components/story/story.css\\\";\\n:root { font-family: system-ui, -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, \\\"Segoe UI\\\", sans-serif; }\\n* { box-sizing: border-box; }\\nbody { margin: 0; }\\na { color: inherit; }\\nbutton, select { font: inherit; }\\nimport type { Metadata } from \\\"next\\\";\\nimport { copy } from \\\"@/content/copy\\\";\\nimport \\\"./globals.css\\\";\\n\\nexport const metadata: Metadata = copy.metadata;\\nexport default function RootLayout({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\n  return <html lang={copy.lang}><body>{children}</body></html>;\\n}\\n/*\\n  A pose is where one piece is. A formation is a pose for every piece plus how\\n  the whole stack is turned and placed. The scene at rest on a beat is one\\n  formation; moving between beats is a blend of two.\\n*/\\n\\nexport type Vec3 = [number, number, number];\\n\\nexport type Pose = {\\n  position: Vec3;\\n  /** Euler angles in radians, applied in X, Y, Z order. */\\n  rotation: Vec3;\\n  /** Width and height. */\\n  size: [number, number];\\n};\\n\\nexport type Formation = {\\n  pieces: Pose[];\\n  /** How the whole stack is turned, about X then Y, in radians. */\\n  turn: [number, number];\\n  /**\\n   * Where the stack sits on a wide screen, as a share of half the screen:\\n   * 0.5 across is halfway from the middle to the right edge. Narrow screens\\n   * centre it.\\n   */\\n  anchor: [number, number];\\n  /** How much the pieces drift while idle, 0 to 1. */\\n  drift: number;\\n  /** How wide the formation is, so the camera can stand back far enough. */\\n  span: number;\\n  /** How tall it is, for the same reason. */\\n  rise: number;\\n  /** The closest the camera comes to the formation. */\\n  near: number;\\n  /**\\n   * How the view changes while the beat is scrolled through: the turn about\\n   * X, the turn about Y, and the distance, each centred on the formation's\\n   * own.\\n   */\\n  sway: Vec3;\\n  /** The height of the floor under the formation. */\\n  floor: number;\\n  /**\\n   * 1 when the formation stands over the caption's column on a wide screen,\\n   * and not beside the caption, so that it has to be fitted above the text\\n   * on a screen shorter than the one it was framed on. See camera.ts.\\n   */\\n  over: number;\\n};\\n\\nexport const lerp = (a: number, b: number, t: number): number => a + (b - a) * t;\\n\\n/** 0 below `a`, 1 above `b`, and a smooth S-curve in between. */\\nexport function smoothstep(x: number, a: number, b: number): number {\\n  if (a === b) return x < a ? 0 : 1;\\n  const t = Math.min(1, Math.max(0, (x - a) / (b - a)));\\n  return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);\\n}\\n\\nconst clamp01 = (x: number): number => Math.min(1, Math.max(0, x));\\n\\n/** The share of a move over which the pieces set off, one after another. */\\nexport const SPREAD = 0.45;\\n\\n/**\\n * One piece's own progress through a move that is `t` of the way along. The\\n * first piece sets off at once and the last when `spread` of the move has\\n * gone; each then takes the time that is left. With no spread they move as\\n * one.\\n */\\nexport function stagger(t: number, i: number, count: number, spread: number): number {\\n  if (!(spread > 0) || count <= 1) return clamp01(t);\\n  const share = Math.min(spread, 0.95);\\n  const start = (share * i) / (count - 1);\\n  return clamp01((t - start) / (1 - share));\\n}\\n\\n/** A formation with every piece at the origin, to be written into. */\\nexport function blankFormation(count: number): Formation {\\n  return {\\n    pieces: Array.from(\\n      { length: count },\\n      (): Pose => ({ position: [0, 0, 0], rotation: [0, 0, 0], size: [1, 1] }),\\n    ),\\n    turn: [0, 0],\\n    anchor: [0, 0],\\n    drift: 0,\\n    span: 0,\\n    rise: 0,\\n    near: 0,\\n    sway: [0, 0, 0],\\n    floor: 0,\\n    over: 0,\\n  };\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Writes the blend of `a` and `b` into `out` and returns it. It allocates\\n * nothing, so it can run every frame. `a` and `b` must have at least as many\\n * pieces as `out`.\\n *\\n * `each`, when given, is every piece's own progress, so the pieces can move\\n * one after another. What belongs to the whole stack always moves by `t`.\\n */\\nexport function mixFormation(\\n  out: Formation,\\n  a: Formation,\\n  b: Formation,\\n  t: number,\\n  each?: ArrayLike<number>,\\n): Formation {\\n  for (let i = 0; i < out.pieces.length; i++) {\\n    const piece = out.pieces[i];\\n    const from = a.pieces[i];\\n    const to = b.pieces[i];\\n    const own = each ? each[i] : t;\\n    for (let k = 0; k < 3; k++) {\\n      piece.position[k] = lerp(from.position[k], to.position[k], own);\\n      piece.rotation[k] = lerp(from.rotation[k], to.rotation[k], own);\\n    }\\n    piece.size[0] = lerp(from.size[0], to.size[0], own);\\n    piece.size[1] = lerp(from.size[1], to.size[1], own);\\n  }\\n  out.turn[0] = lerp(a.turn[0], b.turn[0], t);\\n  out.turn[1] = lerp(a.turn[1], b.turn[1], t);\\n  out.anchor[0] = lerp(a.anchor[0], b.anchor[0], t);\\n  out.anchor[1] = lerp(a.anchor[1], b.anchor[1], t);\\n  out.drift = lerp(a.drift, b.drift, t);\\n  out.span = lerp(a.span, b.span, t);\\n  out.rise = lerp(a.rise, b.rise, t);\\n  out.near = lerp(a.near, b.near, t);\\n  for (let k = 0; k < 3; k++) out.sway[k] = lerp(a.sway[k], b.sway[k], t);\\n  out.floor = lerp(a.floor, b.floor, t);\\n  out.over = lerp(a.over, b.over, t);\\n  return out;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * How far the view has swung from where the formations put it: about X,\\n * about Y, and in distance. `dwell` is how far through its rest `a` is; `b`\\n * has not begun its own.\\n */\\nexport function swing(\\n  out: Vec3,\\n  a: Formation,\\n  b: Formation,\\n  t: number,\\n  dwell: number,\\n): Vec3 {\\n  for (let k = 0; k < 3; k++) {\\n    out[k] = lerp(a.sway[k] * (dwell - 0.5), b.sway[k] * -0.5, t);\\n  }\\n  return out;\\n}\\n\\n/** A beat may keep moving while it rests, driven only by its scroll dwell. */\\nexport type BeatFormation = { rest: Formation; within?: Formation };\\n\\n/** Writes a beat's current resting pose without changing its source data. */\\nexport function atRest(out: Formation, beat: BeatFormation, dwell: number): Formation {\\n  const progress = Number.isFinite(dwell) ? Math.min(1, Math.max(0, dwell)) : 0;\\n  return mixFormation(out, beat.rest, beat.within ?? beat.rest, progress);\\n}\\n/** Surface, light and text colours can change without changing the story. */\\nexport type Look = {\\n  id: string;\\n  name?: string;\\n  ground: string;\\n  ink: string;\\n  faint: string;\\n  accent: string;\\n  surface: { color: string; roughness: number; glows: boolean };\\n  lights: { ambient: number; key: number; fill: number; rim: number };\\n  floor: \\\"matte\\\" | \\\"none\\\";\\n  shadows: boolean;\\n};\\n\\nexport function parseLook(value: string | null | undefined, looks: readonly Look[], defaultId?: string): string {\\n  const fallback = looks.find((look) => look.id === defaultId) ?? looks[0];\\n  if (!fallback) throw new Error(\\\"A story needs at least one look\\\");\\n  return looks.find((look) => look.id === value)?.id ?? fallback.id;\\n}\\n\\nexport function lookFromSearch(search: string, looks: readonly Look[], defaultId?: string): string {\\n  return parseLook(new URLSearchParams(search).get(\\\"look\\\"), looks, defaultId);\\n}\\n\\n/** Preserves unrelated parameters and leaves the default look out of the URL. */\\nexport function searchWithLook(search: string, id: string, defaultId: string): string {\\n  const params = new URLSearchParams(search);\\n  if (id === defaultId) params.delete(\\\"look\\\");\\n  else params.set(\\\"look\\\", id);\\n  const text = params.toString();\\n  return text ? `?${text}` : \\\"\\\";\\n}\\nimport type { Pose, Vec3 } from \\\"./formation.ts\\\";\\nimport { rand } from \\\"./rand.ts\\\";\\n\\ntype Size = [number, number];\\nfunction pieces(count: number, size: Size, place: (index: number) => { position: Vec3; rotation?: Vec3 }): Pose[] {\\n  if (!Number.isInteger(count) || count < 0) throw new RangeError(\\\"piece count must be a non-negative integer\\\");\\n  if (!size.every((value) => Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0)) throw new RangeError(\\\"piece size must be finite and positive\\\");\\n  return Array.from({ length: count }, (_, index) => {\\n    const pose = place(index);\\n    return { position: pose.position, rotation: pose.rotation ?? [0, 0, 0], size: [...size] };\\n  });\\n}\\n\\nexport function stack(count: number, size: Size, options: { offset?: Vec3 } = {}): Pose[] {\\n  const offset = options.offset ?? [0.018, 0.012, 0.07];\\n  return pieces(count, size, (index) => ({ position: offset.map((value) => value * (index - (count - 1) / 2)) as Vec3 }));\\n}\\n\\nexport function fan(count: number, size: Size, options: { angle?: number; radius?: number; depth?: number } = {}): Pose[] {\\n  const angle = options.angle ?? 1.4;\\n  const radius = options.radius ?? size[1] * 0.7;\\n  return pieces(count, size, (index) => {\\n    const turn = count > 1 ? (index / (count - 1) - 0.5) * angle : 0;\\n    return {\\n      position: [Math.sin(turn) * radius, (Math.cos(turn) - 1) * radius, (index - (count - 1) / 2) * (options.depth ?? 0.04)],\\n      rotation: [0, 0, -turn],\\n    };\\n  });\\n}\\n\\nexport function ring(count: number, size: Size, options: { radius?: number; start?: number } = {}): Pose[] {\\n  const radius = options.radius ?? Math.max(size[1], (count * size[0] * 1.1) / (2 * Math.PI));\\n  return pieces(count, size, (index) => {\\n    if (count === 1) return { position: [0, 0, 0] };\\n    const angle = (options.start ?? Math.PI / 2) + (index * 2 * Math.PI) / count;\\n    return { position: [Math.cos(angle) * radius, Math.sin(angle) * radius, 0], rotation: [0, 0, angle - Math.PI / 2] };\\n  });\\n}\\n\\nexport function grid(count: number, size: Size, options: { columns?: number; gap?: number } = {}): Pose[] {\\n  if (!Number.isInteger(count) || count < 0) throw new RangeError(\\\"piece count must be a non-negative integer\\\");\\n  const columns = options.columns ?? Math.max(1, Math.ceil(Math.sqrt(count)));\\n  if (!Number.isInteger(columns) || columns < 1) throw new RangeError(\\\"columns must be a positive integer\\\");\\n  const rows = Math.ceil(count / columns);\\n  const gap = options.gap ?? 0.2;\\n  return pieces(count, size, (index) => {\\n    const row = Math.floor(index / columns);\\n    const inRow = Math.min(columns, count - row * columns);\\n    return { position: [(index % columns - (inRow - 1) / 2) * (size[0] + gap), ((rows - 1) / 2 - row) * (size[1] + gap), 0] };\\n  });\\n}\\n\\nexport function row(count: number, size: Size, options: { gap?: number } = {}): Pose[] {\\n  return grid(count, size, { columns: Math.max(1, count), gap: options.gap });\\n}\\n\\nexport function scatter(count: number, size: Size, options: { seed?: number; span?: number; rise?: number; depth?: number } = {}): Pose[] {\\n  const seed = options.seed ?? 1;\\n  const span = options.span ?? size[0] * Math.sqrt(count) * 1.7;\\n  const rise = options.rise ?? size[1] * Math.sqrt(count) * 0.8;\\n  return pieces(count, size, (index) => ({\\n    position: [(rand(index, seed) - 0.5) * span, (rand(index, seed + 1) - 0.5) * rise, (rand(index, seed + 2) - 0.5) * (options.depth ?? 1.5)],\\n    rotation: [(rand(index, seed + 3) - 0.5) * 0.7, (rand(index, seed + 4) - 0.5) * 0.7, (rand(index, seed + 5) - 0.5) * 1.2],\\n  }));\\n}\\n\\n/** Width and height centred on the formation's origin, enclosing every rotated\\n * corner. The camera aims at that origin, so an offset layout needs room for\\n * its farthest edge on both sides, even when its bounding box is smaller.\\n */\\nexport function measure(poses: readonly Pose[]): { span: number; rise: number } {\\n  if (!poses.length) return { span: 0, rise: 0 };\\n  let left = Infinity, right = -Infinity, bottom = Infinity, top = -Infinity;\\n  for (const pose of poses) {\\n    const [rx, ry, rz] = pose.rotation;\\n    const a = Math.cos(rx), b = Math.sin(rx), c = Math.cos(ry), d = Math.sin(ry), e = Math.cos(rz), f = Math.sin(rz);\\n    for (const x of [-pose.size[0] / 2, pose.size[0] / 2]) {\\n      for (const y of [-pose.size[1] / 2, pose.size[1] / 2]) {\\n        const px = pose.position[0] + c * e * x - c * f * y;\\n        const py = pose.position[1] + (a * f + b * e * d) * x + (a * e - b * f * d) * y;\\n        left = Math.min(left, px);\\n        right = Math.max(right, px);\\n        bottom = Math.min(bottom, py);\\n        top = Math.max(top, py);\\n      }\\n    }\\n  }\\n  return {\\n    span: 2 * Math.max(Math.abs(left), Math.abs(right)),\\n    rise: 2 * Math.max(Math.abs(bottom), Math.abs(top)),\\n  };\\n}\\n# Asset provenance and use\\n\\nAll five public assets below were authored by the evaluation coordinator\\nfor this fictional derivative on 11 October 2026. });\\nconst require = createRequire(import.meta.url);\\nconst next = require.resolve(\\\"next/dist/bin/next\\\");\\nconst [{ story }, { looks }] = await Promise.all([\\n probes = await readFile(join(root, \\\"content/checks.json\\\"), \\\"utf8\\\").then(JSON.parse).catch(error => {\\n  if (error.code === \\\"ENOENT\\\") return {};\\n  throw error;\\n});\\nconst matrix = checkMatrix(contract, probes).filter(entry => !options.only || String(options.only).split(\\\",\\\").includes(entry.name));\\nif (!matrix.length) throw new Error(\\\"--only selected no Set();\\nlet cancelled = false;\\n\\nfunction stop(child, signal = \\\"SIGTERM\\\") {\\n  if (!child?.pid || child.exitCode !== null) return;\\n  try {\\n    if (process.platform === \\\"win32\\\") child.kill(signal);\\n    else process.kill(-child.pid, signal);\\n  }\\n  catch (error) { if (error.code !== \\\"ESRCH\\\") throw error; }\\n}\\n\\nfunction launch(args, logName) {\\n  const log = createWriteStream(join(output, logName));\\n  const child = spawn(process.execPath, args, {\\n    cwd: root, detached: process.platform !== \\\"win32\\\",\\n    env: { ...process.env, NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED: \\\"1\\\" }, stdio: [\\\"ignore\\\", \\\"pipe\\\", \\\"pipe\\\"],\\n  });\\n  children.add(child);\\n  manifest.commands.push([process.execPath, ...args]);\\n  child.stdout.on(\\\"data\\\", chunk => log.write(chunk));\\n  child.stderr.on(\\\"data\\\", chunk => log.write(chunk));\\n  const done = new Promise(resolveDone => {\\n    child.on(\\\"error\\\", error => { log.write(`${error.stack}\\\\n`); });\\n    child.on(\\\"close\\\", (code, signal) => {\\n      children.delete(child);\\n      log.end(() => resolveDone({ code, signal }));\\n    });\\n  });\\n  return { child, done };\\n}\\n\\nasync function run(args, logName, timeout = 180_000) {\\n  const processRun = launch(args, logName);\\n  const timer = setTimeout(() => stop(processRun.child), \\\"127.0.0.1\\\", ok); });\\n  const port = server.address().port;\\n  await new Promise((ok, fail) => server.close(error => error ? fail(error) : ok()));\\n  return port;\\n}\\n\\nasync function report?.problems ?? [\\\"No browser report; read this case's log\\\"] });\\n    await save();\\n  }\\n  manifest.complete = true;\\n  manifest.finished = new Date().toISOString();\\n  await save();\\n  const failed = manifest.results.filter(result => result.code !== 0 || result.problems.length);\\n  console.log(`${matrix.length - failed.length}/${matrix.length} checks passed. On a narrow one the\\n  caption takes the lower part of the screen, by an amount that differs from\\n  beat to beat and language to language, so the scene is fitted into the band\\n  that is left between the site header and the caption.\\n*/\\n\\n/** What the page shell measures for the scene, in pixels. */\\nexport type Stage = {\\n  viewport: number;\\n  /** Height of what is pinned to the top of the screen: the story's own bar. */\\n  header: number;\\n  /** Height of each beat's caption when it is pinned, in beat order. */\\n  captions: number[];\\n};\\n\\n/** Room left around a formation, as a share of its size. */\\nconst MARGIN = 1.15;\\n\\n/** No band or room is ever treated as thinner than this share of the screen. */\\nconst THINNEST = 0.15;\\n\\n/**\\n * How far back the camera must stand for a formation `span` units wide and\\n * `rise` units tall to fit, and never closer than `nearest`. `shareX` and\\n * `shareY` are how much of the screen's width and height the formation has\\n * to itself: 1 when it is centred and nothing covers it.\\n */\\nexport function cameraDistance(\\n  span: number,\\n  rise: number,\\n  aspect: number,\\n  shareX: number,\\n  shareY: number,\\n  fovDegrees: number,\\n  nearest: number,\\n): number {\\n  if (!(aspect > 0)) return nearest;\\n  const heightPerUnitOfDistance = 2 * Math.tan((fovDegrees * Math.PI) / 360);\\n  const forWidth =\\n    (span * MARGIN) / (heightPerUnitOfDistance * aspect * Math.max(THINNEST, shareX));\\n  const forHeight =\\n    (rise * MARGIN) / (heightPerUnitOfDistance * Math.max(THINNEST, shareY));\\n  return Math.max(nearest, forWidth, forHeight);\\n}\\n\\n/** Half the width and half the height the camera sees at a distance. */\\nexport function viewHalf(\\n  distance: number,\\n  fovDegrees: number,\\n  aspect: number,\\n): [number, number] {\\n  const halfHeight = distance * Math.tan((fovDegrees * Math.PI) / 360);\\n  return [halfHeight * aspect, halfHeight];\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * The share of the screen a formation has to itself when it is anchored off\\n * centre by `anchor`, a share of half the screen.\\n */\\nexport function roomBeside(anchor: number): number {\\n  return Math.max(THINNEST, 1 - Math.abs(anchor));\\n}\\n\\n/** The height of screen the framing on a wide screen was tuned on, in pixels. */\\nexport const TUNED_HEIGHT = 900;\\n\\n/** A formation fitted to less room than it was framed for is never drawn smaller than this. */\\nconst SMALLEST = 0.4;\\n\\n/**\\n * On a wide screen most formations stand beside the caption. One that\\n * stands over it instead was framed on a screen `TUNED_HEIGHT` tall. A\\n * caption is as many pixels tall on a shorter screen, so it takes more of\\n * it, and the formation would run into the text. This is how much smaller\\n * to draw the formation there: by as much as the band between the header\\n * and the caption has shrunk, as a share of the screen. It is 1 on a screen\\n * that tall or taller.\\n */\\nexport function overScale(viewport: number, header: number, caption: number): number {\\n  const tuned = (TUNED_HEIGHT - header - caption) / TUNED_HEIGHT;\\n  if (!(viewport > 0) || !(tuned > 0)) return 1;\\n  const now = (viewport - header - caption) / viewport;\\n  return Math.min(1, Math.max(SMALLEST, now / tuned));\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * And where to put it: the anchor, a share of half the screen's height,\\n * that keeps the formation at the same place within that band as `anchor`\\n * gave it on the screen it was framed on.\\n */\\nexport function overAnchor(\\n  anchor: number,\\n  viewport: number,\\n  header: number,\\n  caption: number,\\n): number {\\n  const tunedBand = TUNED_HEIGHT - header - caption;\\n  if (!(viewport > 0) || viewport >= TUNED_HEIGHT || !(tunedBand > 0)) return anchor;\\n  const within = (TUNED_HEIGHT * (0.5 - anchor / 2) - header) / tunedBand;\\n  const centre = header + within * Math.max(0, viewport - header - caption);\\n  return 1 - (2 * centre) / viewport;\\n}\\n\\n/** The share of the screen's height left between the header and a caption. */\\nexport function bandShare(viewport: number, header: number, caption: number): number {\\n  if (!(viewport > 0)) return 1;\\n  return Math.min(1, Math.max(THINNEST, (viewport - header - caption) / viewport));\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * On a narrow screen a tall caption can leave a band thinner than the\\n * thinnest the scene is framed for, and the pieces would then stand over the\\n * header and the text. This is how much smaller to draw them so that they\\n * keep to the band: 1 where the band is thick enough, and 0 where so little\\n * is left that they should not be drawn at all.\\n */\\nexport function bandFit(viewport: number, header: number, caption: number): number {\\n  if (!(viewport > 0)) return 1;\\n  const fit = (viewport - header - caption) / viewport / THINNEST;\\n  if (fit >= 1) return 1;\\n  return fit < SMALLEST ? 0 : fit;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * How far above the middle of the screen the middle of that band is, as a\\n * share of the screen's height. The scene is raised by this much.\\n */\\nexport function stageLift(viewport: number, header: number, caption: number): number {\\n  if (!(viewport > 0)) return 0;\\n  return (caption - header) / (2 * viewport);\\n}\\n\\\"use client\\\";\\nimport { useEffect, useMemo, useRef, type ReactNode, type RefObject } from \\\"react\\\";\\nimport { useFrame } from \\\"@react-three/fiber\\\";\\nimport * as THREE from \\\"three\\\";\\nimport { atRest, blankFormation, lerp, mixFormation, swing, type Formation, type Pose, type Vec3 } from \\\"@/lib/story/formation\\\";\\nimport { bandFit, bandShare, cameraDistance, overAnchor, overScale, roomBeside, stageLift, viewHalf, type Stage } from \\\"@/lib/story/camera\\\";\\nimport { pieceOnScreen } from \\\"@/lib/story/overlay\\\";\\nimport { beatsOf } from \\\"@/lib/story/story\\\";\\nimport type { Look } from \\\"@/lib/story/looks\\\";\\nimport type { Controls } from \\\"@/lib/story/controls\\\";\\nimport type { Frame } from \\\"./conductor\\\";\\nimport { createFaces } from \\\"./faces\\\";\\nimport { faceMaterial } from \\\"./face-material\\\";\\nimport type { PieceProps, StoryBundle } from \\\"./types\\\";\\n\\nexport const FOV = 38;\\nexport const CAMERA_Z = 12;\\nexport type Pointer = { x: number; y: number };\\nfunction Card({ material, look }: PieceProps) {\\n  // Bias the thin box sides behind printed faces to avoid MSAA edge speckles.\\n  return <><mesh castShadow receiveShadow frustumCulled={false}><boxGeometry args={[1, 1, 0.045]} /><meshStandardMaterial color={look.surface.color} roughness={look.surface.roughness} polygonOffset polygonOffsetFactor={1} polygonOffsetUnits={1} /></mesh><mesh position={[0, 0, 0.03]} material={material} frustumCulled={false}><planeGeometry args={[1, 1]} /></mesh></>;\\n}\\nexport function Pieces({ bundle, frameRef, stageRef, pointerRef, formationRef, controls, look, coarse, still, rootRef, onError, children }: {\\n  bundle: StoryBundle; frameRef: RefObject<Frame>; stageRef: RefObject<Stage>; pointerRef: RefObject<Pointer>;\\n  formationRef: RefObject<Formation>; controls: Controls; look: Look; coarse: boolean; still: boolean;\\n  rootRef: RefObject<HTMLDivElement | null>; onError: () => void; children?: ReactNode;\\n}) {\\n  const group = useRef<THREE.Group>(null);\\n  const groups = useRef<(THREE.Group | null)[]>([]);\\n  const count = bundle.story.pieces;\\n  const beats = useMemo(() => beatsOf(bundle.story), [bundle.story]);\\n  const from = useRef(blankFormation(count));\\n  const to = useRef(blankFormation(count));\\n  const swung = useRef<Vec3>([0, 0, 0]);\\n  const projected = useRef([0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]);\\n  const overlayPose = useRef<Pose>({ position: [0, 0, 0], rotation: [0, 0, 0], size: [1, 1] });\\n  const blank = useMemo(() => {\\n    const value = look.surface.glows ? 0 : 255;\\n    const texture = new THREE.DataTexture(new Uint8Array([value, value, value, 255]), 1, 1);\\n    texture.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace; texture.needsUpdate = true; return texture;\\n  }, [look]);\\n  const materials = useMemo(() => Array.from({ length: count }, () => faceMaterial(look, blank)), [look, blank, count]);\\n  const faces = useRef<ReturnType<typeof createFaces> | null>(null);\\n  const links = useRef<HTMLElement[]>([]);\\n  useEffect(() => {\\n    const store = createFaces(bundle.faces, look, coarse);\\n    faces.current = store;\\n    links.current = Array.from(rootRef.current?.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(\\\"[data-story-link]\\\") ?? []);\\n    return () => { faces.current = null; store.dispose(); for (const link of links.current) { link.style.visibility = \\\"hidden\\\"; link.tabIndex = -1; } };\\n  }, [bundle.faces, look, coarse, rootRef]);\\n  useEffect(() => () => { for (const face of materials) face.material.dispose(); blank.dispose(); }, [materials, blank]);\\n\\n  useFrame(state => {\\n    const frame = frameRef.current;\\n    const source = bundle.formations[beats[frame.from].id];\\n    const target = bundle.formations[beats[frame.to].id];\\n    const dwell = still ? (frame.dwell < 0.5 ? 0 : 1) : frame.dwell;\\n    // Resolve the dwell first. Travel leaves the completed within formation.\\n    atRest(from.current, source, dwell);\\n    atRest(to.current, target, frame.to === frame.from ? dwell : 0);\\n    const now = mixFormation(formationRef.current, from.current, to.current, frame.t, frame.each);\\n    const stage = stageRef.current;\\n    const caption = lerp(stage.captions[frame.from] ?? 0, stage.captions[frame.to] ?? 0, frame.t);\\n    const shareX = frame.narrow ? 1 : roomBeside(now.anchor[0]);\\n    const shareY = frame.narrow ? bandShare(stage.viewport, stage.header, caption) : roomBeside(now.anchor[1]);\\n    const swing3 = swing(swung.current, from.current, to.current, frame.t, still ? 0.5 : frame.dwell);\\n    const over = frame.narrow ? 0 : now.over;\\n    const smaller = over > 0 ? lerp(1, overScale(stage.viewport, stage.header, caption), over) : 1;\\n    const anchorY = over > 0 ? lerp(now.anchor[1], overAnchor(now.anchor[1], stage.viewport, stage.header, caption), over) : now.anchor[1];\\n    const fit = frame.narrow ? bandFit(stage.viewport, stage.header, caption) : 1;\\n    const aspect = state.size.width / state.size.height;\\n    const z = cameraDistance(now.span, now.rise, aspect, shareX, shareY, FOV, now.near + swing3[2]) / (smaller * (fit || 1));\\n    state.camera.position.z = z;\\n    const [halfWidth, halfHeight] = viewHalf(z, FOV, aspect);\\n    const stack = group.current;\\n    if (!stack) return;\\n    stack.visible = fit > 0;\\n    stack.rotation.set(now.turn[0] + swing3[0] - (still ? 0 : pointerRef.current.y * 0.04), now.turn[1] + swing3[1] + (still ? 0 : pointerRef.current.x * 0.07), 0);\\n    if (frame.narrow) stack.position.set(0, 2 * halfHeight * stageLift(stage.viewport, stage.header, caption), 0);\\n    else stack.position.set(now.anchor[0] * halfWidth, anchorY * halfHeight, 0);\\n    stack.updateMatrix();\\n    try { faces.current?.draw(); } catch { onError(); return; }\\n    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {\\n      const piece = groups.current[i];\\n      const pose = now.pieces[i];\\n      if (!piece) continue;\\n      const drift = still ? 0 : Math.sin(frame.time * 0.5 + i) * now.drift * 0.025;\\n      const lift = !still && controls.get(\\\"piece\\\") === i ? 0.025 : 0;\\n      piece.position.set(pose.position[0], pose.position[1] + drift, pose.position[2] + lift);\\n      piece.rotation.set(...pose.rotation);\\n      piece.scale.set(pose.size[0], pose.size[1], 1);\\n      const store = faces.current;\\n      if (store) materials[i].set(store.get(beats[frame.from].id, i), store.get(beats[frame.to].id, i), frame.each[i]);\\n    }\\n    for (let i = 0; i < (bundle.links?.length ?? 0); i++) {\\n      const link = links.current[i];\\n      const def = bundle.links![i];\\n      if (!link) continue;\\n      const active = fit > 0 && frame.from === frame.to && beats[frame.from].id === def.beat;\\n      const mesh = groups.current[def.piece];\\n      const pose = now.pieces[def.piece];\\n      if (mesh && pose) {\\n        overlayPose.current.position[0] = mesh.position.x;\\n        overlayPose.current.position[1] = mesh.position.y;\\n        overlayPose.current.position[2] = mesh.position.z + 0.03;\\n        overlayPose.current.rotation = pose.rotation;\\n        overlayPose.current.size = pose.size;\\n      }\\n      const p = active && mesh && pose ? pieceOnScreen(projected.current, overlayPose.current, { distance: z, fov: FOV, aspect, fromStack: stack.matrix.elements }) : null;\\n      const visible = p && p.every(Number.isFinite) && Math.abs(p[0]) < 1.2 && Math.abs(p[1]) < 1.2;\\n      link.style.visibility = visible ? \\\"visible\\\" : \\\"hidden\\\"; link.tabIndex = visible ? 0 : -1;\\n      if (visible && p) {\\n        const w = state.size.width / 2; const h = state.size.height / 2;\\n        link.style.transform = `matrix(${p[2] * w / 50},${-p[3] * h / 50},${-p[4] * w / 50},${p[5] * h / 50},${w + p[0] * w},${h - p[1] * h})`;\\n      }\\n    }\\n  });\\n  const Shape = bundle.Piece ?? Card;\\n  return <group ref={group}>{Array.from({ length: count }, (_, index) => <group key={index} ref={node => { groups.current[index] = node; }}><Shape index={index} material={materials[index].material} look={look} /></group>)}{children}</group>;\\n}\\nimport { TRAVEL } from \\\"../lib/story/timeline.ts\\\";\\n\\n/** Empty ignore values mean no exception. A nonempty value is an explicit regex. */\\nexport function optionalPattern(value) {\\n  return value ? new RegExp(String(value)) : null;\\n}\\n\\nexport function expectedRequestReason({ url, resourceType, failure }, { ignore = null, block = null, noJavaScript = false, pageUrl } = {}) {\\n  if (block?.test(url)) return \\\"intentionally blocked request\\\";\\n  if (ignore?.test(url)) return \\\"explicit ignore pattern\\\";\\n  if (noJavaScript && resourceType === \\\"script\\\" && [\\\"csp\\\", \\\"net::ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CSP\\\"].includes(failure)) {\\n    try {\\n      const request same-origin Next.js script preload\\\";\\n      }\\n    } catch { /* Invalid URLs cannot qualify for this narrow exception. */ }\\n  }\\n  return null;\\n}\\n\\nexport function requestProblem({ url, status, failure, resourceType }, options = {}) {\\n  if (expectedRequestReason({ url, resourceType, failure }, options)) return null;\\n  if (failure) return `request failed (${failure}): ${url}`;\\n  return status >= 400 ? `HTTP ${status}: ${url}` : null;\\n}\\n\\nexport function storyContract(story, looks) {\\n  const ids = story.chapters.flatMap(chapter => chapter.beats.map(beat => beat.id));\\n  if (!ids.length || new Set(ids).size !== ids.length || ids.some(id => !/^[\\\\w.-]+$/.test(id))) {\\n    throw new Error(\\\"The check contract needs unique, nonempty beat IDs\\\");\\n  }\\n  const lookIds = looks.map(look => look.id);\\n  if (!lookIds.length || new Set(lookIds).size !== lookIds.length) throw new Error(\\\"The check contract needs unique look IDs\\\");\\n  return { ids, lookIds };\\n}\\n\\n/** The contract comes from content, never from whatever the first browser happened to render. */\\nexport function checkMatrix({ ids, lookIds }, probes = {}) {\\n  const common = [`--expect-beats=${ids.length}`, `--expect-ids=${ids.join(\\\",\\\")}`, \\\"--beats\\\", \\\"--inside=.story-control\\\"];\\n  const matrix = [];\\n  for (const look of lookIds) {\\n    const expected = [...common, `--expect-look=${look}`];\\n    matrix.push(\\n      { name: `${look}-wide`, look, flags: [...expected, \\\"--size=1440x900\\\", \\\"--expect-scene=on\\\", `${look}-no-webgl`, look, flags: [...expected, \\\"--size=1440x900\\\", \\\"--no-webgl\\\", \\\"--expect-scene=off\\\"] },\\n    );\\n  }\\n  matrix.push(\\n    { name: \\\"no-js\\\", look: lookIds[0], flags: [...common, \\\"--size=1440x900\\\", \\\"--no-js\\\", \\\"--expect-selector=h1\\\", \\\"--hidden=canvas,.story-look-switch,.story-control\\\"] },\\n    { name: \\\"context-loss\\\", look: lookIds[0], flags: [...common, \\\"--size=1440x900\\\", \\\"--lose-context\\\", \\\"--expect-scene=off\\\"] },\\n  );\\n  if (lookIds.length > 1) matrix.push({ name: \\\"look-switch\\\", look: browsers'\",\"aggregated_output\":\"\",\"exit_code\":null,\"status\":\"in_progress\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_13\",\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/bash time.\\n(Use `node --trace-warnings ...` to show where the warning was created)\\n\\n> storyfolio-site@0.1.0 browsers\\n> playwright-core install chromium\\n\\n(node:21) [UNDICI-EHPA] Warning: node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/14-metadata-and-og-images.md\\\"\",\"aggregated_output\":\"---\\ntitle: How to lazy load Client Components and libraries\\nnav_title: Lazy Loading\\ndescription: Lazy load imported libraries and React Components to improve your application's loading performance.\\n---\\n\\n{/* The content of this doc is shared between the app and pages router. You can use the `<PagesOnly>Content</PagesOnly>` component to add content that is specific to the Pages Router. Any shared content should not be wrapped in a component. */}\\n\\n[Lazy loading](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/Performance/Lazy_loading) in Next.js helps improve the initial loading performance of an application by decreasing the amount of JavaScript needed to render a route.\\n\\n<AppOnly>\\n\\nIt allows you to defer loading of **Client Components** and imported libraries, and only include them in the client bundle when they're needed. For example, you might want to defer loading a modal until a user clicks to open it.\\n\\nThere are two ways you can implement lazy loading in Next.js:\\n\\n1. Using [Dynamic Imports](#nextdynamic) with `next/dynamic`\\n2. Using [`React.lazy()`](https://react.dev/reference/react/lazy) with [Suspense](https://react.dev/reference/react/Suspense)\\n\\nBy default, Server Components are automatically [code split](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Glossary/Code_splitting), and you can use [streaming](/docs/app/guides/streaming) to progressively send pieces of UI from the server to the client. Lazy loading applies to Client Components.\\n\\n## `next/dynamic`\\n\\n`next/dynamic` is a composite of [`React.lazy()`](https://react.dev/reference/react/lazy) and [Suspense](https://react.dev/reference/react/Suspense). It behaves the same way in the `app` and `pages` directories to allow for incremental migration.\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\n### Importing Client Components\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/page.js\\\"\\n'use client'\\n\\nimport { useState } from 'react'\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\n// Client Components:\\nconst ComponentA = dynamic(() => import('../components/A'))\\nconst ComponentB = dynamic(() => import('../components/B'))\\nconst ComponentC = dynamic(() => import('../components/C'), { ssr: false })\\n\\nexport default function ClientComponentExample() {\\n  const [showMore, setShowMore] = useState(false)\\n\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      {/* Load immediately, but in a separate client bundle */}\\n      <ComponentA />\\n\\n      {/* Load on demand, only when/if the condition is met */}\\n      {showMore && <ComponentB />}\\n      <button onClick={() => setShowMore(!showMore)}>Toggle</button>\\n\\n      {/* Load only on the client side */}\\n      <ComponentC />\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n> **Note:** When a Server Component dynamically imports a Client Component, automatic [code splitting](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Glossary/Code_splitting) is currently **not** supported.\\n\\n### Skipping SSR\\n\\nWhen using `React.lazy()` and Suspense, Client Components will be [prerendered](https://github.com/reactwg/server-components/discussions/4) (SSR) by default.\\n\\n> **Note:** `ssr: false` option will only work for Client Components, move it into Client Components ensure the client code-splitting working properly.\\n\\nIf you want to disable prerendering for a Client Component, you can use the `ssr` option set to `false`:\\n\\n```jsx\\nconst ComponentC = dynamic(() => import('../components/C'), { ssr: false })\\n```\\n\\n### Importing Server Components\\n\\nIf you dynamically import a Server Component, only the Client Components that are children of the Server Component will be lazy-loaded - not the Server Component itself.\\nIt will also help preload the static assets such as CSS when you're using it in Server Components.\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/page.js\\\"\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\n// Server Component:\\nconst ServerComponent = dynamic(() => import('../components/ServerComponent'))\\n\\nexport default function ServerComponentExample() {\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <ServerComponent />\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n> **Note:** `ssr: false` option is not supported in Server Components. You will see an error if you try to use it in Server Components.\\n> `ssr: false` is not allowed with `next/dynamic` in Server Components. Please move it into a Client Component.\\n\\n### Loading External Libraries\\n\\nExternal libraries can be loaded on demand using the [`import()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Operators/import) function. This example uses the external library `fuse.js` for fuzzy search. The module is only loaded on the client after the user types in the search input.\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/page.js\\\"\\n'use client'\\n\\nimport { useState } from 'react'\\n\\nconst names = ['Tim', 'Joe', 'Bel', 'Lee']\\n\\nexport default function Page() {\\n  const [results, setResults] = useState()\\n\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <input\\n        type=\\\"text\\\"\\n        placeholder=\\\"Search\\\"\\n        onChange={async (e) => {\\n          const { value } = e.currentTarget\\n          // Dynamically load fuse.js\\n          const Fuse = (await import('fuse.js')).default\\n          const fuse = new Fuse(names)\\n\\n          setResults(fuse.search(value))\\n        }}\\n      />\\n      <pre>Results: {JSON.stringify(results, null, 2)}</pre>\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Adding a custom loading component\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/page.js\\\"\\n'use client'\\n\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\nconst WithCustomLoading = dynamic(\\n  () => import('../components/WithCustomLoading'),\\n  {\\n    loading: () => <p>Loading...</p>,\\n  }\\n)\\n\\nexport default function Page() {\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      {/* The loading component will be rendered while  <WithCustomLoading/> is loading */}\\n      <WithCustomLoading />\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n### Importing Named Exports\\n\\nTo dynamically import a named export, you can return it from the Promise returned by [`import()`](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Operators/import) function:\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"components/hello.js\\\"\\n'use client'\\n\\nexport function Hello() {\\n  return <p>Hello!</p>\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/page.js\\\"\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\nconst ClientComponent = dynamic(() =>\\n  import('../components/hello').then((mod) => mod.Hello)\\n)\\n```\\n\\n## Magic Comments\\n\\nNext.js supports magic comments to control how dynamic imports are handled by the bundler. These comments work with dynamic `import()`, `require()`, `require.resolve()`, and `new Worker()` expressions.\\n\\n> **Good to know:** Magic comments do not work with static `import` statements (`import x from 'y'`). They only work with dynamic expressions.\\n\\n### `webpackIgnore` / `turbopackIgnore`\\n\\nUse these comments to skip bundling a dynamic import. The import expression will be left as-is in the output, useful for runtime-only modules:\\n\\n```js\\n// Skip bundling - import happens at runtime\\nconst runtime = await import(/* webpackIgnore: true */ 'runtime-module')\\n\\n// Turbopack-specific variant\\nconst plugin = await import(/* turbopackIgnore: true */ pluginPath)\\n\\n// Also works with require\\nconst mod = require(/* webpackIgnore: true */ 'runtime-module')\\n```\\n\\n### `turbopackOptional` (Turbopack only)\\n\\nUse this comment to suppress build errors when a module might not exist. The import will still throw at runtime if the module is missing:\\n\\n```js\\n// No build error if './optional-feature' doesn't exist\\n// Runtime will throw MODULE_NOT_FOUND if executed\\nconst feature = await import(/* turbopackOptional: true */ './optional-feature')\\n\\n// Also works with require\\nconst mod = require(/* turbopackOptional: true */ './optional-module')\\n```\\n\\nThis is useful for:\\n\\n- Conditional features that may not be installed\\n- Plugin systems where modules are optional\\n- Gradual migrations where some files may not exist yet\\n\\n> **Good to know:** `webpackOptional` is not supported. Use `turbopackOptional` instead when using Turbopack.\\n\\n</AppOnly>\\n\\n<PagesOnly>\\n\\n## `next/dynamic`\\n\\n`next/dynamic` is a composite of [`React.lazy()`](https://react.dev/reference/react/lazy) and [Suspense](https://react.dev/reference/react/Suspense). It behaves the same way in the `app` and `pages` directories to allow for incremental migration.\\n\\nIn the example below, by using `next/dynamic`, the header component will not be included in the page's initial JavaScript bundle. The page will render the Suspense `fallback` first, followed by the `Header` component when the `Suspense` boundary is resolved.\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\nconst DynamicHeader = dynamic(() => import('../components/header'), {\\n  loading: () => <p>Loading...</p>,\\n})\\n\\nexport default function Home() {\\n  return <DynamicHeader />\\n}\\n```\\n\\n> **Good to know**: In `import('path/to/component')`, the path must be explicitly written. It can't be a template string nor a variable. Furthermore the `import()` has to be inside the `dynamic()` call for Next.js to be able to match webpack bundles / module ids to the specific `dynamic()` call and preload them before rendering. `dynamic()` can't be used inside of React rendering as it needs to be marked in the top level of the module for preloading to work, similar to `React.lazy`.\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\n### With named exports\\n\\nTo dynamically import a named export, you can return it from the [Promise](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Promise) returned by [`import()`](https://github.com/tc39/proposal-dynamic-import#example):\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"components/hello.js\\\"\\nexport function Hello() {\\n  return <p>Hello!</p>\\n}\\n\\n// pages/index.js\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\nconst DynamicComponent = dynamic(() =>\\n  import('../components/hello').then((mod) => mod.Hello)\\n)\\n```\\n\\n### With no SSR\\n\\nTo dynamically load a component on the client side, you can use the `ssr` option to disable server-rendering. This is useful if an external dependency or component relies on browser APIs like `window`.\\n\\n```jsx\\n'use client'\\n\\nimport dynamic from 'next/dynamic'\\n\\nconst DynamicHeader = dynamic(() => import('../components/header'), {\\n  ssr: false,\\n})\\n```\\n\\n### With external libraries\\n\\nThis example uses the external library `fuse.js` for fuzzy search. The module is only loaded in the browser after the user types in the search input.\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport { useState } from 'react'\\n\\nconst names = ['Tim', 'Joe', 'Bel', 'Lee']\\n\\nexport default function Page() {\\n  const [results, setResults] = useState()\\n\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <input\\n        type=\\\"text\\\"\\n        placeholder=\\\"Search\\\"\\n        onChange={async (e) => {\\n          const { value } = e.currentTarget\\n          // Dynamically load fuse.js\\n          const Fuse = (await import('fuse.js')).default\\n          const fuse = new Fuse(names)\\n\\n          setResults(fuse.search(value))\\n        }}\\n      />\\n      <pre>Results: {JSON.stringify(results, null, 2)}</pre>\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n</PagesOnly>\\n---\\ntitle: not-found.js\\ndescription: API reference for the not-found.js file.\\n---\\n\\nNext.js provides two conventions to handle not found cases:\\n\\n- **`not-found.js`**: Used when you call the [`notFound`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/not-found) function in a route segment.\\n- **`global-not-found.js`**: Used to define a global 404 page for unmatched routes across your entire app. This is handled at the routing level and doesn't depend on rendering a layout or page.\\n\\n## `not-found.js`\\n\\nThe **not-found** file is used to render UI when the [`notFound`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/not-found) function is thrown within a route segment. Along with serving a custom UI, Next.js will return a `200` HTTP status code for streamed responses, and `404` for non-streamed responses (see [Status Codes](/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/loading#status-codes) for details about SEO).\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/not-found.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\n\\nexport default function NotFound() {\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <h2>Not Found</h2>\\n      <p>Could not find requested resource</p>\\n      <Link href=\\\"/\\\">Return Home</Link>\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/blog/not-found.js\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\n\\nexport default function NotFound() {\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <h2>Not Found</h2>\\n      <p>Could not find requested resource</p>\\n      <Link href=\\\"/\\\">Return Home</Link>\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\nIn the [component hierarchy](/docs/app/getting-started/project-structure#component-hierarchy), `not-found.js` renders between `loading.js` and `page.js`. It is wrapped by the `<Suspense>` boundary from `loading.js` and the error boundary from `error.js` in the same segment.\\n\\n> **Good to know**: The default not found UI follows the operating system's color scheme via `prefers-color-scheme` and does not read an app-level theme (such as a class or `data-theme` attribute on `<html>`). Because it renders inside your root layout, the quickest way to match an explicit theme is to add a higher-specificity rule pair in your global stylesheet, scoped to your theme selector — for example `html[data-theme='light'] body` and `html[data-theme='dark'] body`. For full control over the markup, provide your own `not-found.js`.\\n\\n## `global-not-found.js` (experimental)\\n\\nThe `global-not-found.js` file lets you define a 404 page for your entire application. Unlike `not-found.js`, which works at the route level, this is used when a requested URL doesn't match any route at all. Next.js **skips rendering** and directly returns this global page.\\n\\nThe `global-not-found.js` file bypasses your app's normal rendering, which means you'll need to import any global styles, fonts, or other dependencies that your 404 page requires. This includes your theme: because `global-not-found.js` bypasses your layout, the OS color scheme is the only signal the default UI sees, so apply your theme (class or attribute) inside this file.\\n\\n> **Good to know**: A smaller version of your global styles, and a simpler font family could improve performance of this page.\\n\\n`global-not-found.js` is useful when you can't build a 404 page using a combination of `layout.js` and `not-found.js`. This can happen in two cases:\\n\\n- Your app has multiple root layouts (e.g. `app/(admin)/layout.tsx` and `app/(shop)/layout.tsx`), so there's no single layout to compose a global 404 from.\\n- Your root layout is defined using top-level dynamic segments (e.g. `app/[country]/layout.tsx`), which makes composing a consistent 404 page harder.\\n\\nTo enable it, add the `globalNotFound` flag in `next.config.ts`:\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"next.config.ts\\\"\\nimport type { NextConfig } from 'next'\\n\\nconst nextConfig: NextConfig = {\\n  experimental: {\\n    globalNotFound: true,\\n  },\\n}\\n\\nexport default nextConfig\\n```\\n\\nThen, create a file in the root of the `app` directory: `app/global-not-found.js`:\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/global-not-found.tsx\\\" switcher\\n// Import global styles and fonts\\nimport './globals.css'\\nimport { Inter } from 'next/font/google'\\nimport type { Metadata } from 'next'\\n\\nconst inter = Inter({ subsets: ['latin'] })\\n\\nexport const metadata: Metadata = {\\n  title: '404 - Page Not Found',\\n  description: 'The page you are looking for does not exist.',\\n}\\n\\nexport default function GlobalNotFound() {\\n  return (\\n    <html lang=\\\"en\\\" className={inter.className}>\\n      <body>\\n        <h1>404 - Page Not Found</h1>\\n        <p>This page does not exist.</p>\\n      </body>\\n    </html>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/global-not-found.js\\\" switcher\\n// Import global styles and fonts\\nimport './globals.css'\\nimport { Inter } from 'next/font/google'\\n\\nconst inter = Inter({ subsets: ['latin'] })\\n\\nexport const metadata = {\\n  title: '404 - Page Not Found',\\n  description: 'The page you are looking for does not exist.',\\n}\\n\\nexport default function GlobalNotFound() {\\n  return (\\n    <html lang=\\\"en\\\" className={inter.className}>\\n      <body>\\n        <h1>404 - Page Not Found</h1>\\n        <p>This page does not exist.</p>\\n      </body>\\n    </html>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\nUnlike `not-found.js`, this file must return a full HTML document, including `<html>` and `<body>` tags.\\n\\n## Reference\\n\\n### Props\\n\\n`not-found.js` or `global-not-found.js` components do not accept any props.\\n\\n> **Good to know**: In addition to catching expected `notFound()` errors, the root `app/not-found.js` and `app/global-not-found.js` files handle any unmatched URLs for your whole application. This means users that visit a URL that is not handled by your app will be shown the exported UI.\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\n### Data Fetching\\n\\nBy default, `not-found` is a Server Component. You can mark it as `async` to fetch and display data:\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/not-found.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\nimport { headers } from 'next/headers'\\n\\nexport default async function NotFound() {\\n  const headersList = await headers()\\n  const domain = headersList.get('host')\\n  const data = await getSiteData(domain)\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <h2>Not Found: {data.name}</h2>\\n      <p>Could not find requested resource</p>\\n      <p>\\n        View <Link href=\\\"/blog\\\">all posts</Link>\\n      </p>\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/not-found.jsx\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\nimport { headers } from 'next/headers'\\n\\nexport default async function NotFound() {\\n  const headersList = await headers()\\n  const domain = headersList.get('host')\\n  const data = await getSiteData(domain)\\n  return (\\n    <div>\\n      <h2>Not Found: {data.name}</h2>\\n      <p>Could not find requested resource</p>\\n      <p>\\n        View <Link href=\\\"/blog\\\">all posts</Link>\\n      </p>\\n    </div>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\nIf you need to use Client Component hooks like `usePathname` to display content based on the path, you must fetch data on the client-side instead.\\n\\n### Metadata\\n\\nFor `global-not-found.js`, you can export a `metadata` object or a [`generateMetadata`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/generate-metadata) function to customize the `<title>`, `<meta>`, and other head tags for your 404 page:\\n\\n> **Good to know**: Next.js automatically injects `<meta name=\\\"robots\\\" content=\\\"noindex\\\" />` for pages that return a 404 status code, including `global-not-found.js` pages.\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/global-not-found.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport type { Metadata } from 'next'\\n\\nexport const metadata: Metadata = {\\n  title: 'Not Found',\\n  description: 'The page you are looking for does not exist.',\\n}\\n\\nexport default function GlobalNotFound() {\\n  return (\\n    <html lang=\\\"en\\\">\\n      <body>\\n        <div>\\n          <h1>Not Found</h1>\\n          <p>The page you are looking for does not exist.</p>\\n        </div>\\n      </body>\\n    </html>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/global-not-found.js\\\" switcher\\nexport const metadata = {\\n  title: 'Not Found',\\n  description: 'The page you are looking for does not exist.',\\n}\\n\\nexport default function GlobalNotFound() {\\n  return (\\n    <html lang=\\\"en\\\">\\n      <body>\\n        <div>\\n          <h1>Not Found</h1>\\n          <p>The page you are looking for does not exist.</p>\\n        </div>\\n      </body>\\n    </html>\\n  )\\n}\\n```\\n\\n## Version History\\n\\n| Version   | Changes                                             |\\n| --------- | --------------------------------------------------- |\\n| `v15.4.0` | `global-not-found.js` introduced (experimental).    |\\n| `v13.3.0` | Root `app/not-found` handles global unmatched URLs. |\\n| `v13.0.0` | `not-found` introduced.                             |\\n---\\ntitle: Metadata and OG images\\ndescription: Learn how to add metadata to your pages and create dynamic OG images.\\nrelated:\\n  title: API Reference\\n  description: Learn more about the Metadata APIs mentioned in this page.\\n  links:\\n    - app/api-reference/functions/generate-metadata\\n    - app/api-reference/functions/generate-viewport\\n    - app/api-reference/functions/image-response\\n    - app/api-reference/file-conventions/metadata\\n    - app/api-reference/file-conventions/metadata/app-icons\\n    - app/api-reference/file-conventions/metadata/opengraph-image\\n    - app/api-reference/file-conventions/metadata/robots\\n    - app/api-reference/file-conventions/metadata/sitemap\\n    - app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/htmlLimitedBots\\n---\\n\\nThe Metadata APIs can be used to define your application metadata for improved SEO and web shareability and include:\\n\\n1. [The static `metadata` object](#static-metadata)\\n2. [The dynamic `generateMetadata` function](#generated-metadata)\\n3. Special [file conventions](/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/metadata) that can be used to add static or dynamically generated [favicons](#favicons) and [OG images](#static-open-graph-images).\\n\\nWith all the options above, Next.js will automatically generate the relevant `<head>` tags for your page, which can be inspected in the browser's developer tools.\\n\\nThe `metadata` object and `generateMetadata` function exports are only supported in Server Components.\\n\\n## Default fields\\n\\nThere are two default `meta` tags that are always added even if a route doesn't define metadata:\\n\\n- The [meta charset tag](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/HTML/Element/meta#attr-charset) sets the character encoding for the website.\\n- The [meta viewport tag](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/HTML/Viewport_meta_tag) sets the viewport width and scale for the website to adjust for different devices.\\n\\n```html\\n<meta charset=\\\"utf-8\\\" />\\n<meta name=\\\"viewport\\\" content=\\\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\\\" />\\n```\\n\\nThe other metadata fields can be defined with the `Metadata` object (for [static metadata](#static-metadata)) or the `generateMetadata` function (for [generated metadata](#generated-metadata)).\\n\\n## Static metadata\\n\\nTo define static metadata, export a [`Metadata` object](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/generate-metadata#metadata-object) from a static [`layout.js`](/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/layout) or [`page.js`](/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/page) file. For example, to add a title and description to the blog route:\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/blog/layout.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport type { Metadata } from 'next'\\n\\nexport const metadata: Metadata = {\\n  title: 'My Blog',\\n  description: '...',\\n}\\n\\nexport default function Layout() {}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/blog/layout.js\\\" switcher\\nexport const metadata = {\\n  title: 'My Blog',\\n  description: '...',\\n}\\n\\nexport default function Layout() {}\\n```\\n\\nYou can view a full list of available options, in the [`generateMetadata` documentation](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/generate-metadata#metadata-fields).\\n\\n## Generated metadata\\n\\nYou can use [`generateMetadata`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/generate-metadata) function to `fetch` metadata that depends on data. For example, to fetch the title and description for a specific blog post:\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/blog/[slug]/page.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport type { Metadata, ResolvingMetadata } from 'next'\\n\\ntype Props = {\\n  params: Promise<{ slug: string }>\\n  searchParams: Promise<{ [key: string]: string | string[] | undefined }>\\n}\\n\\nexport async function generateMetadata(\\n  { params, searchParams }: Props,\\n  parent: ResolvingMetadata\\n): Promise<Metadata> {\\n  const slug = (await params).slug\\n\\n  // fetch post information\\n  const post = await fetch(`https://api.vercel.app/blog/${slug}`).then((res) =>\\n    res.json()\\n  )\\n\\n  return {\\n    title: post.title,\\n    description: post.description,\\n  }\\n}\\n\\nexport default function Page({ params, searchParams }: Props) {}\\n```\\n\\n\",\"exit_code\":0,\"status\":\"completed\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.started\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_15\",\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/bash -lc \\\"cat > /tmp/build-spine.py <<'PY'\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\ndef put(path, text): Path(path).write_text(text)\\nput('content/story.ts', '''import type { StoryDef, BeatIdOf } from \\\\\\\"../lib/story/story.ts\\\\\\\";\\n// Stop 4: the three approved spine beats. Any shared content should not be wrapped in a component. */}\\n\\n`<Link>` is a React component that extends the HTML `<a>` element to provide [prefetching](/docs/app/getting-started/linking-and-navigating#prefetching) and client-side navigation between routes. It is the primary way to navigate between routes in Next.js.\\n\\nBasic usage:\\n\\n<AppOnly>\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/page.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\n\\nexport default function Page() {\\n  return <Link href=\\\"/dashboard\\\">Dashboard</Link>\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/page.js\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\n\\nexport default function Page() {\\n  return <Link href=\\\"/dashboard\\\">Dashboard</Link>\\n}\\n```\\n\\n</AppOnly>\\n\\n<PagesOnly>\\n\\n```tsx filename=\\\"pages/index.tsx\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\n\\nexport default function Home() {\\n  return <Link href=\\\"/dashboard\\\">Dashboard</Link>\\n}\\n```\\n\\n```jsx filename=\\\"pages/index.js\\\" switcher\\nimport Link from 'next/link'\\n\\nexport default function Home() {\\n  return <Link href=\\\"/dashboard\\\">Dashboard</Link>\\n}\\n```\\n\\n</PagesOnly>\\n\\n## Reference\\n\\nThe following props can be passed to the `<Link>` component:\\n\\n<PagesOnly>\\n\\n| Prop                        | Example                  | Type              | Required |\\n| --------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------- | -------- |\\n| [`href`](#href-required)    | `href=\\\"/dashboard\\\"`      | String or Object  | Yes      |\\n| [`as`](#as)                 | `as=\\\"/post/abc\\\"`         | String or Object  | - 'reduce' : 'no-preference' });\\n    const page = await context.newPage(); const requests=[];\\n    page.on('request', request => requests.push(request.url()));\\n    await page.goto(\\\"'`${base}/#transition`, { waitUntil:'\\\"'networkidle' }); await page.waitForTimeout(2500);\\n    const canvas = await page.locator('canvas').count();\\n    const requestedScene = requests.filter(url => sceneChunks.some(chunk => url.endsWith('/'+chunk)));\\n    if (mode==='desktop') {\\n      assert.equal(canvas,1); await context.close();\\n  }\\n  await writeFile(\\\"'`${output}/report.json`, JSON.stringify({ sceneChunks, results, limits:'\\\"'Automated Chromium checks; no real phone, mail delivery, publication or social-platform validation.' },null,2)+'\\\\\\\\n');\\n  console.log(JSON.stringify(results));\\n} finally { await browser?.close(); -lc \\\"cat > docs/story/review.md <<'EOF'\\n# Spine review — ready for the person's look choice\\n\\n11 October 2026. This is stop 4, not approval of the completed site.\\n\\nThe local working spine contains Ada Mensah, Reading transitions and Code\\nbeside evidence, carried by the same six row cards in all three looks.\\nUse the appearance switch to compare Bench paper, Instrument paper and\\nReview sheet while scrolling. No choice has yet been received.\\n\\n## Checks performed\\n\\n| Command / inspection | Result |\\n|---|---|\\n| \\\"'`npm ci` | Registry request blocked; installation recovered with `npm ci --offline` (392 locked packages) |\\n| `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/tmp/storyfolio-browser-cache npm run browsers` | Passed |\\n| `npm test` | 131 passed, 0 failed |\\n| `npm run typecheck` | Passed |\\n| `npm run lint` | Passed |\\n| `npm run check -- --jobs=3 --wait=1000` with supplied browser cache and libraries | Fresh production build; full matrix, 18/18 passed |\\n| `node scripts/check-spine.mjs checks/2026-10-11T01-55-22-591Z/supplemental` with the same browser environment | Static chunk loading, desktop navigation/deep link, keyboard reachability, supplied assets, email/CV attributes and missing-page checks passed |\\n| Visual inspection | All 93 matrix screenshots opened through labelled contact sheets; representative original desktop and phone images also opened. Supplemental screenshots opened separately. |\\n\\nProduction evidence: `checks/2026-10-11T01-55-22-591Z/`. Its manifest is\\ncomplete and reports no browser problems. The tracked compact record is\\n`check-results.json`; logs, individual PNGs and labelled contact sheets\\nremain locally under that check folder. Previous runs were stopped for\\nvisual corrections or terminated before completion; they are not acceptance\\nruns. The final checker still defaults to sequential execution; optional\\nbatching preserved the entire matrix within the environment'\\\"'s runtime limit.\\n\\n## Observations\\n\\nThe desktop table stays framed beside the prose, separates into Sleep,\\nMeasure and Transmit teaching pairs, then returns to its row formation.\\nThe third appearance changes the palette to cool paper and blue annotations;\\nit keeps the same object and factual labels. All looks keep readable text\\nand labelled explanatory drawings. The short-desktop switch is above the\\nrows. Phone header, chapter links, switch and opening now have separate\\nspace. Large static diagrams are readable without the canvas.\\n\\nPhone and reduced-motion contexts each mounted zero canvases and requested\\nzero identified scene chunks. Desktop mounted one canvas and fetched that\\nchunk. Direct arrival at \\\"'`#transition` and navigation to `#review` settled\\nwith the matching active scene chapter.
